I'll say the shortest I can, and in such a very synthetic formula. The Rural Development Programme was approved by the European Commission on 14 December 2014. This whole year, which was for the preparation of implementing regulations and laws, is now in such a very intense formula that 28 regulations have already been drawn up and 14 regulations are to be issued. As far as the Rural Development Programme is concerned, the funds at our disposal for the 2014-2020 period are 13.5 billion funds from the European Union, 13.5 billion is the entire budget of the development programme. Within this budget, 8.6 billion are funds from the European Union budget, the European Agricultural Fund, rural development and 4.9 million are national contributions, public funds. Let me remind you that 25% has been transferred from the second pillar from the development programme to the first pillar of direct payment, and I would like to remind you that the budget for the period 2014-2020, in this established, negotiated form, was 13.5 billion, so much money in the period 20072013 from the European Union, from the European Fund. As far as the design of the Rural Development Programme is concerned, before I discuss individual actions, the types of operations that are being implemented must be said very, very briefly, that we have 15 actions, this is the design of the Rural Development Programme, there are 15 actions, 35 sub-measures and 42 types of operations. We can comment briefly that we want it to be as simple as possible, but we need to learn this new nomenclature. There used to be four axes and actions, but these types of operations, sub-actions and actions are also in this formula in the regional operational programmes, it was about harmonising the nomenclature. This is the first case. The second point is that I would divide the funds available under the Rural Development Programme into several such areas. The first area is the development of farms, the second area is the strengthening of entrepreneurship, the third area is territorial development, the fourth area - knowledge transfer and innovation and the fifth area - production quality support. I'll say it like that. In March we also start implementing a very important component, component D: modernisation of agricultural holdings. Component D is a component, it's like an old modernization, i.e. machines, tools, technological process, tractors. This is the first time we've started this in March. And note: in March will also be repeated the component for piglets, i.e. ABC, so-called. ABC component for modernisation, i.e. for piglets, for the production of dairy cows and for meat cattle. So in March we have a young farmer and as if all types of farm modernisation operations. These are the basic ones, the most important ones planned in the first quarter. While we are prepared and here is the declaration, it is a commitment both to all the implementing rules and to the restructuring agencies, which are ready to implement it. However, as far as the young farmer is concerned, we are waiting for final confirmation from the European Commission so that this whole administrative procedure can be implemented. As far as farm development is concerned, in subsequent periods we have payments for farmers [[♪ The bell ♪]] delivering small farms, we have the creation of producer groups and producer organisations in June, we have investments that recreate the potential of agricultural production also in the middle of the year. Several of them have already mentioned, I would add that we actually have 12 agencies and institutions that are engaged today in promoting Polish exports and acting in quasi-market logic, competing with each other, which is not the best solution. We know that they should rather create some kind of synergy in order to support this export and these investments in real terms. So we would like to create an efficient, flexible instrument to support exports and support foreign investment, which will accumulate the experience and potential of these institutions, which are already active in the area of Polish administration at central and regional level. These are instruments that we have within the framework of the Polish Information and Foreign Investment Agency, but also funds dedicated to promotions in PARP, also in the Ministry of Development through trade and investment promotion departments, to a certain extent in the Ministry of Agriculture, mentioned this also by the Member, the Agricultural Market Agency or finally the National Centre for Research and Development, in the Ministry of Environment through GreenEvo programmes. They are all engaged in the promotion of the Polish economy today, but they do so in an uncoordinated way. So we want to build a system that is more efficient and more coherent. To do this, we want to set up a new agency to promote exports and investment or, please, consider it as a working name, an agency to support exports and we want to subpoena its development minister. This new agency will be built to a large extent on the basis of today's PAIiIZ, but also for comparison, and so that you have the scale we assume, today PAIiIZ has a budget of about PLN 10 million, and we want this new agency to have a budget between PLN 200 million and PLN 250 million. These are not the means drawn out of the sleeve, these are the means that, after summing up these instruments that I mentioned earlier, we really have. The legal form of this new instrument remains to be determined: whether it is an executive agency, a public legal person or a public limited liability company. In particular, this first form is supported by the possibility of saving funds for an entity grant in the Budget Act, the granting of a targeted grant for substantive actions by the Ministry of Development or by other ministries, as well as the obtaining of a targeted grant for financing projects involving European funds. We assume that this new institution may be established on 1 January 2017. In order to achieve these objectives, we are aware that we need to build a whole network of cooperation between the national partners of the agencies and foreign partners. We want to encourage and integrate local authorities and investor and exporter service centres into this national network. They should all have a uniform logo and uniform lines of action, should be easily recognizable, we should create such a clear system of one window so that the investor, the exporter knows clearly who he can turn to and can report to. Finally, a network of foreign partners. We are, of course, proposing that this network should be set up again on the basis of the trade promotion and investment departments under the responsibility of the Minister of Development today, especially those who are doing well in this area. We are also reviewing these departments at the moment, and we are looking at which of them actually meet the goals that are in front of such institutions. We will strengthen our presence abroad by opening new facilities. We have already decided to open new offices in Serbia and Vietnam while reviewing them, as I said, and these institutions which are not in strategic places for the Polish economy, these departments will be liquidated. In addition, we want to support institutions on eight important, key markets for the Polish economy, i.e. We are talking about Germany, the United States, Ireland, the UK, China, and perhaps also the countries of Southeast Asia. The Agency will maintain and even strengthen the industry promotion system. This is the direction we're talking about. If we want to follow the best patterns, as the Germans do, as the French do, it is this industry promotion that is this axis of export support and we will focus especially on those 12 sectors that we know about so far, those where Poland has the greatest competitive advantages, i.e. food, IT, furniture, automotive parts, yacht construction, cosmetics as well as so-called. creative industries. [[♪ The bell ♪]] We will finally continue programmes dedicated to specific markets such as Go China, Go Africa, Go India, perhaps Go Iran, the next direction. Finally, we intend to continue the organisation, as I said, of the economic missions, but we are convinced that they can be more effective, and we intend to work for this greater effectiveness. As I said, the new agency and the new instrument will be launched early next year. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-14-sejm-08-2.u88	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Mrs. Member! Thank you so much for asking that question. The preservation of the Polish shipbuilding industry is crucial for us. The Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Shipping has already taken action on the revitalisation of the shipbuilding industry. Well, it has already been prepared and submitted to the list of work of the Council of Ministers a draft of the draft Act on Activation of the Marine Industry and Complementary Production. The aim of the project is to create a legal, organisational and financial framework necessary for the creation of industrial, scientific and financial activities in Poland aimed at the development of existing and the creation of new Polish business entities. There is an agreement on entities with the capacity or capacity to achieve, conclude and implement full contracts for the construction of ships on sea and inland, including warranty and service services, as a final product created on the territory of the Republic of Poland. This also involves development-oriented activities and the creation of an entity in complementary production. This is also the issue of polonisation of the supplier chain. In addition, the proposed solutions should stimulate the development of R &amp; D centres developing new innovative types of ships, particularly in terms of ecological solutions currently being enforced by international rules, namely the use of alternative low carbon fuels, solutions for cleanliness of exhaust gases and ballasts, hybrid or fully electric units. The need to develop this project results from the need to ensure competitive operating rules - on the basis of so-called. level playing field - Polish ship industry and complementary production in conditions of fierce and unequal competition in international markets. In particular, it is about competition from strong support for Eastern Asian ship production, but not only because the neighbours also do it. As a result, economic and legal conditions will be created to activate the shipbuilding industry and complementary production in Poland. Detailed project solutions. The proposal provides for exemptions for VAT in the scope of complementary shipbuilding. These facilities will cover both the final product, ready to operate the ship, and all its components produced in the supply chain. I would like to draw your attention to the fact that the finished ship produced by the Polish shipyard is not subject to VAT, so we will try to work in this direction, so that also suppliers, so-called. makers list, the supplier chain was not subject to VAT, which must be financed by poor shipyards for a period of preparation and production of a ship which usually lasts 2 years. With regard to corporation tax, CIT, the project provides for a choice for the yard between the tax, so far, CIT and the flat-rate tax on the value of the sold production of 1%. I wanted to point out that the so-called. Special production will not be able to enjoy this kind of facilitation. The choice of this form of taxation will be made by the trader making a declaration. If a declaration is made, it will be possible to change the form of taxation no earlier than 3 years later. A similar mechanism is provided for in the current Act of 24 August 2006 on tonnage tax. In addition, the project provides for a mechanism to cover the special economic zone of land in which shipbuilding and complementary production are carried out. The above-mentioned facilitations are of a particular nature and will cover only the construction of ready-to-use ships, but will not include ship repairs. Why? Because the cost-effectiveness of ship repairs... The margins range between 12 and 25% And we don't think repair yards need that much help. Of course, this does not preclude the use of the above mentioned. facilitating the construction of new ships by companies specialising in their repairs as a rule, as is currently the case. It is worth noting that the Enterprise Development Programme, adopted by the Council of Ministers on 8 April 2014 contains a comprehensive system of support for enterprises with a particular focus on innovation. An integral part of this programme is the document entitled: National Intelligent Specialization (KIS) identifying areas of R &amp; D and innovation. In this context, it should be noted that the Steering Committee on the National Intelligent Specializations decided to create a new national specialization Mon. Innovating marine technologies in the field of specialized... [[♪ The bell ♪]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-14-sejm-08-2.u89	Finish it. At the moment, under the aegis of the Ministry of Development, which is responsible for the entire KIS, work will be carried out to determine the exact description of the maritime intelligent specialisation in question. It is worth mentioning that it consists of three thematic areas: design, construction and conversion of vessels, design, construction and reconstruction of marine and coastal structures and processes and equipment used for logistics based on maritime and inland transport. Companies working in the shipbuilding sector will be able to submit applications for funding under the Operational Programme (PO IR) The Commission therefore In order to be accepted, the application will be evaluated in terms of enlisting in this or other KISs. ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-14-sejm-08-2.u112	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I wish I could see the Minister of Family, Labour and Social Policy. The Constitutional Tribunal of 21 October 2014 in the Case of Signatorys. Act K 38/13 ruled that Article. Article 17 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1b of the Family Benefits Act is incompatible with Article. Article 32 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1st Constitution. It is therefore unconstitutional. The case concerns the differentiation of the right to provide care for persons taking care of a disabled person after the age laid down in that provision has been completed due to the moment of disability. I quote the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal: Application of the article. Article 17 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1b The Family Benefits Act as a criterion for identifying beneficiaries of care provision leads to such differentiation within the right to care benefits, which loses its constitutional justification. This is the case when a person requiring care changes his status to include him or her as a child within the meaning of the legislator. Under the Family Benefits Act, the criterion distinguishing such a category is the age of 18, possibly the age of 25 during school or higher education. Crossing such a age limit makes it possible to speak only of one group of similar entities from that point on. It is then created by carers of adults with disabilities. There is no constitutional justification for the diversity of entities belonging to this category, e.g. as regards the amount of care benefits they receive. The Constitutional Tribunal pointed out the negative effects of the contested provision. He stated that the guardians of adult disabled persons must be treated as members of the same class by the legislator and therefore cannot in principle be treated differently and ordered the judgment to be settled without undue delay. On 21 October 2015, a year has passed since the delivery of the judgment and no solutions have been put in place to address the situation of guardians. I may add, with a moment's notice, that the carers of adults with disabilities demand the restoration of the rights acquired for the care provided as a substitute - a benefit for carers who were deprived of the amendment of the Family Benefits Act of 7 December 2012 dividing them into carers for children with disabilities and carers for adults with disabilities. Adult people with disabilities are currently in Poland about 150 thousand. The reduction in child benefit after the age of 18 and the treatment of a disabled child as an adult is not justified because most of these persons, most of these children, whether they are 15 or 15 years old 30, or even 50, according to parents, are still children who require constant care, constant support. Therefore, these maintenance costs do not change at all. Parents who are 18 years of age have no more time and facilities, e.g. to get back to work. On the contrary, the cost of living for an adult disabled person is very often much higher than the cost of living for a disabled child, as with age there are additional diseases that also require both additional medications and additional involvement and rehabilitation. Responding to the appeal of the disfellowshipped guardians of adults with disabilities - because not only are modern Members, but I suspect that all Members are being e-mailed at the moment by the parents and guardians of adults with disabilities asking what happened to the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal, because this judgment, as I mentioned, has not yet been implemented, the provisions of this judgment - the Poselski Club The modern one asks whether the Minister of Family, Labour and Social Policy intends to execute the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 21 October 2014 in the case of signage. K 38/13 and whether legislative work is being carried out on the implementation of this judgment. I'll just add that there were a lot of charges to the previous ruling crew that she didn't execute about a dozen or so, in fact, 48, if I recall correctly, judgments of the Constitutional Tribunal. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Today is an excellent opportunity to change this situation, improve it and make these judgments begin to be executed. ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-14-sejm-08-2.u140	Mr. Marshal! Gentlemen of Ministers! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Communications Commission with Poles abroad a draft law amending the Polish Charter Act and some other laws. The Polish Charter Act was prepared by the government of Jarosław Kaczyński and entered into force in 2008. It's been nearly eight years since then. A good time to sum up the work of this law so far, carry out an evaluation and also listen to the voices of our fellow countrymen from the East, for whom we have prepared and adopted this law. And it was after such a deep consultation with NGOs that the Commission launched a legislative initiative with the intention of introducing a relatively deep revision. We propose the following changes. The last year pointed out that there is no legal basis for our countrymen, holders of the Polish Charter in extraordinary situations, in health and life-threatening situations, to be able to evacuate from areas at risk in order to give them effective assistance. During the last year, as I said, we had to evacuate Poles from Donbass twice and one of the problems that the Polish state encountered at that time was just a lack of legal basis for effective assistance. We are introducing a provision which, while respecting international law, will create such an opportunity. Thanks to it, the holders of the Polish Charter will be treated on similar terms as Polish citizens in health and life-threatening situations. The second important change we are proposing is a change consisting in making it easier to settle in the Republic of Poland. Firstly, we propose to exempt from all fees the holders of the Polish Charter, both those who apply for Polish citizenship through the consulates of the Republic of Poland and those who come to Poland, want to receive a permanent residence card and then citizenship. We propose that every holder of the Polish Charter may, upon arrival in Poland, be free of charge, automatically, if he so wishes, receive a permanent residence card and then after a year, if he so wishes, receive Polish citizenship. During this year, we propose that during this year he should receive support from the Polish state, financial support which could be used to finance the rental of the apartment, to learn Polish language or to train professionally. I think this is an extremely important issue, because I would like to remind you that even in its current formula, the Law on repatriation covers only our compatriots living in the Asian part of the Soviet Union, and Poles living in the area of today's Belarus, Ukraine is not able to benefit from repatriation. The solution proposed by us to all those who would like to come to Poland permanently will create this possibility. It can also be pointed out here that from the point of view of the Polish state, from the point of view of the demographic problems with which we meet, this solution is also beneficial, because it is probably a much better solution if we could solve demographic deficiencies or demographic problems, using Poles, people of Polish origin from the East, not people who come from completely different cultural circles. Another important change we are proposing is the change in the possibility of applying for the Polish Charter not only, as so far, in the consulates of the Republic of Poland, but also in the voivodship offices. In countries where there is no full democracy, such as: Belarus, the authorities are reluctant to look at those applying for the Polish Charter. For this reason, some have not decided to apply for this document so far. Through the proposed change, we want to create a situation in which such a person, our countryman, arrives, for example. to Bialystok, he could arrange all the formalities at the provincial office and then pick up the Pole's Charter after a certain time without exposing himself to harassment by the Belarusian authorities. Another important change that we are introducing is the exemption from the interview with the consul of people who graduated from school with the Polish language of teaching. This change has not only a practical dimension, and in this way we also raise the prestige of these institutions. There are not many of them, because in Belarus they are two schools, in Ukraine - six, in Lithuania - almost 100, but we should ensure that the prestige, the reputation of these schools is as high as possible. In our view, the change we are proposing is building the prestige of these institutions. Another issue and another change we propose in the amendment is the transfer of the former President of the Council of Ministers to the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. The law is implemented to a large extent by the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, where there are people who are best placed in the problem of the law. It seems that this advice should clearly be assigned to the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. This will also allow for its more efficient and efficient functioning. Finally, I would like to say that all these changes are the result, as I mentioned at the beginning, of the deep consultation that we carried out. We sent questions to over 130 Polish organizations, we also consulted NGOs operating in Poland. Of course, not all expectations have been met. Some of these expectations were contradictory, but it seems to us that these proposals we are making are certainly a way out of meeting the expectations of many Polish communities and a step in the right direction. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-14-sejm-08-2.u143	Thank you. Mr Wojciech Zietniak, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-14-sejm-08-2.u144	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club a position regarding the draft law amending the Polish Charter Act and some other laws. The draft law amending the Polish Charter Act and some other laws aims, on the one hand, to introduce a number of solutions facilitating the colonization of the Polish Charter holders in the territory of the Republic of Poland, among others. through an adaptation package and by facilitating the application for Polish citizenship. On the other hand, provision has been made to correct or clarify existing solutions. Until now, the holders of the Polish Charter have been left to themselves, often without means of life, with minimal possessions, which they managed to bring to Poland. The amendments to the Act are intended to help these people start a new life, adapt to new living conditions and, through State aid, bring them to a state where they will become independent and work and maintain themselves. The most important changes envisaged by the amendment include: the possibility of applying for the Polish Charter through the voivodeship, which will be the second body next to the consul authorized to award the Polish Charter. This is intended to facilitate particularly for citizens of countries where the holders of the Polish Charter can face repression by the authorities. It should be clarified whether the voivodships will take over the powers of consuls in their entirety or whether there are any competences that remain solely within the competence of consuls. Second thing: exempting the holders of the Polish Charter from consular fees for the acceptance and processing of the application for a national visa. So far these fees have been a source of income for consulates. The question is whether, in this case, the consulates will not suffer financially. Introducing the right to assist the consul in situations of life or security of the holders of the Polish Charter, within the competence of the consul, respecting international rights and customs. It is necessary to clarify the nature of this assistance, the form in which it is to be provided, what situations will be deemed to be dangerous to safety and life. Will the consul be able to count on the support of the relevant services? Another issue is the emergence of the role of the old-timer and local self-government units in the article. 8d: Forms, the amount and method of granting aid to a person residing in Poland on the basis of a permanent residence permit obtained on the basis of the Polish Charter and the members of its immediate family by local government units shall be determined by the authorities constituting these units. The question is whether local authorities are aware of the new tasks that will fall on them. Were there consultations with local authorities on the new powers? The old days will take over a lot of tasks related to helping the holders of the Polish Charter. Were there any consultations with the elders or with the county's association of staffing opportunities of the authorities? Financial assistance from the State budget for reimbursement of travel expenses, housing adaptation, professional activation, taking up the education of minors are important for the future - ensuring a decent existence for people coming to Poland. However, consideration should be given to the size of the budgetary reserve from which the resources for these tasks will come. Will this reserve be sufficient? Since 2008, 170 000 have been adopted. applications for the award of the Polish Charter. At the moment, the revised law also provides for assistance for card holders' families. Hence the question of whether the reserve will be sufficient. The new records will certainly encourage more people to apply for this card. We must take into account the growing interest in obtaining the Polish Charter and the financial consequences of all the privileges which the Pole Charter holders make an amendment should be foreseen. The Civic Platform Club, bearing in mind the welfare of Poles abroad, welcomes the draft amendment of the law. In particular, rules facilitating the life of those applying for the Polish Charter, forms of financial assistance enabling them to adapt, start working, adapt to the labour market, among others. by learning Polish language in Poland and all changes going in the right direction, which are intended to facilitate life and provide assistance to those in need, are desirable and accepted. However, some general issues need to be clarified and the financial capacity of the State in terms of the aid granted should be more detailed. It is also necessary to estimate how many beneficiaries of this aid Poland can expect. Thank you very much. I presented the position of the Civic Platform Club. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-14-sejm-08-2.u145	Thank you very much. Mr Adam Andruszkiewicz, Kukiz Poselski Club15, has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-14-sejm-08-2.u146	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Kukiz Club15 on the draft law amending the Polish Charter Act and some other laws. This project was prepared by the Commission for Communication with Poles abroad, which I am Vice-President, and in our opinion the rapid pace of work on amending the law has not translated into the quality of the project. Border and Polonia organisations have had the opportunity to familiarise themselves with the proposed changes and to express their own views on this issue. The Polish Charter Act was introduced in 2007 and after 8 years of validity it was high time to meet the expectations of our compatriots, who often paid attention to the too narrow range of support mechanisms that the Pole Charter holders had received so far. The present bill makes a number of changes, which aim to increase the availability of the Polish Charter for our compatriots living abroad, simplifying their arrival in Poland and acclimatization in the Republic of Poland. It is also important to introduce a quick and clear process of granting Polish citizenship. All Poles who lost Polish citizenship during the war and their descendants who feel Poles should be able to return to the status of a full citizen of the Republic of Poland. The Poselski Kukiz Club15 welcomes the provision introducing the possibility of applying for the Poles' Charter through the voivodeship. However, we want to be very sensitive to the officials granting the document, so that in decision-making they follow a deep understanding of the position of our fellow countrymen in the East, especially during the communism period. It should be remembered that Poles were subjected to severe repression and Sovietisation. These factors must be taken into account in the process of verification of belonging to the Polish people. We also consider it a good solution to abolish consular fees for the adoption and examination of a request for a national visa. In the 2007 Act, there was a provision on the possibility of exemption from such a charge or its refund at a written request, which was widely used, so the Polish State did not have any higher income on this basis. The proposed amendment will therefore not, in our opinion, reduce the revenue of the state and at the same time reduce bureaucracy, which as a Kukiz club15 will always be supported. The same is true of changes concerning the exemption from consular fees for the adoption of the application and the preparation of documentation on the granting of Polish citizenship to people holding the Polish Charter. Another of the changes introduced is the introduction of wide adaptation instruments for holders of the Polish Charter wishing to settle in Poland. Most of the provisions in the article come from the Reparation Act and concern the possibility of covering travel expenses, the partial cost of renting a dwelling, the cost of living or the cost of vocational education and training. We believe that these changes will increase the possibility of settling in the territory of the Republic of Poland holders of the Polish Charter. At this point we would also like to draw attention to the fact that, together with the changes in the Polish Charter, there should be an increase in support for Polish organizations in the East, which will be most responsible for maintaining Polishness in the former territories of the Republic of Poland. Poles who do not decide to come to Poland must have increased care of the Polish state, both diplomatic, material and educational. From this point we want to assure all Poles in the East: we will not forget about you, and we will submit an appropriate draft amendment to the budget on this issue today. At the same time we notice a few dangers, based on past experiences. During the period of validity of the Polish Charter, about 170 thousand were issued. these documents, mainly Poles living in Ukraine, Belarus and Lithuania. In the majority of people receiving the Polish Charter were motivated by the desire to mark their belonging to the Polish people, which is an exact assumption of the applicable rules. Unfortunately, some of the people who became holders of this document were motivated only and exclusively by the desire to exercise the powers conferred on them by this document. The additional powers introduced by this amendment may cause Poles who are illegally applying for the Polish Charter to arrive. In the history of issuing this document, there have been loud cases involving both receiving cards on the basis of false documents and the suspicion of receiving cards as a result of corruption. We also have cases of unworthy behaviour of people who received the Polish Charter. There are cases of people who photographed with flagship flags in Przemyśl and Rzeszów, people who have symbols associated with pro-bander movements in dorms in Lublin and several other such cases. It should be noted that these people, although apparently not identified with the Polish people, and their behaviour was derogatory to the Republic of Poland, did not lose their cards. Therefore, in order to prevent such situations from happening in the future, we have tabled an appropriate amendment in committee work. As a Kukiz club15, we welcome most of the proposed changes, while believing that possible issues will be resolved in committee. We believe that the slogan: We want a repatriate instead of an emigrant, thanks to our joint work we will soon put into practice. Therefore, we will support the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-14-sejm-08-2.u147	Thank you very much. Mr Zbigniew Gryglas, Modern.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-14-sejm-08-2.u148	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! We welcomed the initiative of the Communication Commission with Poles abroad, which aims to amend the law. For us, this is a specific act of historical justice, a form of debt repayment that we have towards our compatriots, who, as a result of many tragic events, tragic our history, exiles, war activities or the shifting of borders of the Republic of Poland, have found themselves outside its territory. The project is not the implementation of a repatriation policy, as the President rightly said, but it can result, according to various estimates, in coming to our country even about 100,000. persons. I would like to point out that this is a proposal primarily for young people and middle-aged people, because I think such people will decide to settle in our country. I will remind you of the basic assumptions of this novel. The holder of the Polish Charter will be able to come to Poland, obtain the right of permanent residence, and will be exempt from the costs which are related to these issues. Moreover, in the first year of his stay in Poland he will also be entitled to finance rent of apartments, Polish language learning and other needs related to adaptation in the Republic of Poland. After a year he will finally be able to obtain Polish citizenship. This is the first such comprehensive solution, from which we are very, very happy. However, we believe that there should be in particular two important issues in the project, two major changes. The first concerns the scope of the award of the Polish Charter. Let me remind you that the Polish Charter is granted to people who are over 65 years of age. We want these people to be offered some opportunities as well. These people, for obvious reasons, will probably not choose to come to our country because it is difficult to decide to change their place of residence when they are 65 years old or older. This is why we want to propose, and we will present this idea in the committee, so that these people can be given the opportunity to finance the purchase of medicines or receive the reimbursement of medical services. These are older people and very often their material situation on the ground does not allow them to afford the medical care they need at this point in time. The second proposal we will be presenting is the question of removing the possibility of obtaining a Pole's Charter by people without Polish origin. I would like to remind you that there is a mechanism in the Act which guarantees the possibility of obtaining a Pole's Charter as a result of a positive opinion of the Polish organization about the activities of such a person in favour of Polish environments. We believe that this possibility, especially when the Polish Charter contains a number of attractive financial decisions, will lead to abuse. We are afraid - and such signals I received - that some of these certificates issued by Polish organizations had no real basis. Despite these proposals, which I hope will be reflected in the final draft of the amendment, we will support this solution as a good solution for Poland as a solution, which is an act of historical justice. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

They are descendants, to whom Poland is obliged and should stand at the highest level to take care of its nation, which for its highest patriotism was thrown on a difficult and very difficult wanderer. This is for the reconstruction of the Polish state after World War I, for the opposition of 17 September 1939 and during World War II and during the Stalinist repressions after its completion Poles could not return to the country over the Vistula from the East or West. The draft law amending the Act on the Polish Charter and amending certain laws aims to introduce a number of solutions facilitating the holders of the Polish Charter to settle in the territory of the Republic of Poland. The Polish People's Party believes that this is a good time to introduce the proposed solutions, which shows this, among others. for 2 years the conflict in Ukraine, still unstable political, social and economic situation has hit Polish citizens strongly. It is important to mention Poles residing in Belarus, where the rules still make it difficult to apply for the Polish Charter. The Act provides that when they encounter problems in their own country, they can apply for the Polish Charter in Poland through the voivodeship. Proposals to amend the Polish Charter Act - and these are good proposals - include: exemption from consular fees for the admission and processing of a national visa application or for the development of documentation on the assignment of Polish citizenship, and for the people from the eastern border these are the dam amounts. The Polish Charter provides for the possibility of providing a permanent residence permit obtained on the basis of the Polish Charter and the members of its immediate family with one-time assistance provided for: reimbursement of the costs of coming to Poland, development and maintenance, partial payment of the costs of renting a housing unit or learning Polish language. It is good that the Act provides for the professional activation of persons residing in Poland for permanent residence obtained on the basis of the Polish Charter. This applies to those who do not have professional qualifications or have professional qualifications which are not useful in the labour market. The possibility of reimbursement of employer's salary, prizes and employment and other insurance contributions is envisaged. The Polish People's Party provides that the proposed changes will have a positive social impact. The changes make it easier for Poles to return to Poland from eastern areas, which has always produced a positive social and national resonance. Due to the migration crisis in Europe, the Polish Charter will have a good impact on the settlement of compatriots in their homeland, whose name is Poland. In Polish society they will be received positively, this is an important element of social awareness of the commitment to Poles from abroad. We refer to good practices, and I would like to remind you that already years ago the slogan: Each municipality receives a Polish family from Kazakhstan, very well received in local and national environments. It can be assumed that this will have a positive economic impact. As experience so far shows, young people want to bind their future with Poland, and people in a mature age want to live in Poland for better or for worse. The Polish People's Party advocates the adoption of the proposal and further work on amending the Polish Charter Act. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-14-sejm-08-2.u191	Thank you, sir. Before we proceed with the questions, please take the floor of Mr. Mark Michalak's Child Rights Officer.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-14-sejm-08-2.u192	Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Ombudsman of the rights of the child vis-à-vis the government's draft law amending the Family and Care Code in print No 164. The natural environment of development and the welfare of the child is the family. It should therefore be surrounded by the necessary protection and support. The application of State intervention measures to the parental authority leading to the taking of a child should take place as a last resort only after the use and use of other less severe measures for the child and family, or only if the child's well-being requires decisive and immediate action. I would like to stress that the protection of the right of the child to be raised in a natural family is one of the priorities of the Ombudsman. Putting a child in foster care is the country's furthest interference in the life of the family and causes a diametric change in the situation of the minor. Therefore, taking such a decision requires a detailed examination of all the circumstances of the case, taking into account the highest order of good and interests of the child. Replacement baking should be used as a means of temporary family assistance only if other activities have not given the desired effect or where, by reason of the circumstances, i.e. The child's health or life is at risk, there is a need for immediate removal of the child from the family. The principle of proportionality of foster care was introduced into the Family and Care Code by the Law of 9 June 2011 on support for family and foster care, in the work on which the Ombudsman actively participated. The Act contains a whole catalogue of tools whose application is intended to reduce child deprivation in the natural environment of the biological family, while stressing the primacy of the right to be raised in the natural family. Family support is possible with the consent of the family and with the active participation of the family. It is primarily realized in the form of working with a family affected by dysfunction. The work of family assistant, support families and day support facilities is essential here. The obligation to support the family provided for by the Act rests with local authorities and government authorities, although some of the support measures indicated by the Law may also be applied by the court - Article. 109 § Point 2 (1) of the Family and Care Code. Applicable provision of the Article. 112 The family and caring code also allows the child to be placed in foster care in emergency situations. Since the introduction into the system of law of the provision of Article. The 112th family and caring code passed four years. This seems to be a sufficient period of time to analyse the judicial practice in this respect. The provisions of the Family and Care Code, constitutional rules and the Convention on the Rights of the Child result from the principle of subsidiarity, taking into account respect for the subjectivity and autonomy of family life, and the fact that the application of measures to interfere with parental authority resulting in the taking away of a child from parents may take place only after using other less vulnerable measures or when the child's well-being requires decisive and immediate action. Therefore, when introducing the provision of Article. 112 The family and caring code, in the wording of the law on family support and foster care, did not seem necessary to express this principle of expressis verbis in the provision that it would be sufficient to leave freedom to courts through an appropriate interpretation of the applicable rules. In a number of cases where the Ombudsman's Office has received a signal or made public by the media, the Ombudsman has initiated proceedings before the courts within the statutory remit and has also followed the proceedings already in progress. The analysis of these cases shows that some decisions to place a child outside the natural family, despite the wide range of activities that can be taken to safeguard the right of the child to be raised in the family, were premature. They thus point to the observed trend towards easier solutions before the more difficult ones. This results in the possibility of harming children and, in some cases, their families. Undoubtedly, the provision of effective help to the family involves certain difficulties, but the appropriate assistance provided by care institutions to the child should focus on improving the functioning of the family, which does not allow proper care for children. I would like to stress that the accuracy of the custody court's decision to take a child from the family and place him in foster care depends not only on the law, but also on the experience of the judge's life and professional life, and on the good relations he has with people and institutions working for families. The lack of confidence in the social partners and sometimes on the part of judges in the lack of psychological, pedagogical, social or medical expertise make decisions that do not always take into account the welfare of the child. Proposed amendment of the Article. 112 The family and caring code does not, in principle, change the existing legal status. It provides clarification and safeguards for the correct direction of interpretation of existing legal standards in judicial practice. The Ombudsman's assessment of the rights of the child also deserves the use by the projector of a condition of serious threat to the welfare of the child if it is necessary to provide the child with an immediate replacement care and to supplement the existing provision on the form of State interference with the parental authority provided for in Article. 109 § Section 2 (1) (4) of the Family and Care Code by expressing expressis verbis the need to exhaust the possibilities that the custody court should exercise before deciding to place the child in foster care. With regard to the proposed Article. 2, Article 112 of the Family and Care Code, I would like to point out that the cases conducted at the Office of the Ombudsman of Children's Rights show that the material problems of the family are one of the factors determining the need to separate the child from the biological parents. I can't confirm, however, that poverty is the only reason for the court's interference with parental authority. Also from information on the results of the Supreme Audit Chamber's audit. The public authorities' actions in a situation of danger of taking children away from their parents in November 2015 show that the assistance provided by social assistance centres was often ineffective, resulting in the removal, in accordance with the decision of the court, of children from biological families and their placement in foster care. This condition was affected, among others. too late the identification of families, multi-problematicity and unwillingness of cooperation between dysfunctional families and centres, not being assisted by a family assistant and working with these families by social workers, who at the same time worked with dozens of other families with various problems, as well as too few daily support facilities and no support families. The reasons for placing children in foster care due to parental malpractice were most often due to alcohol dependence on one or both parents - 62% cases examined by the Supreme Audit Chamber, unemployment - 24%, health problems - 20%, poverty - 15% and family violence - 11% cases examined, and these negative phenomena accumulated. The Ombudsman, after examining the 33 proceedings identified by the NIC, as a result of which the children were placed in foster care, did not find irregularities in the conduct of these proceedings. In the case that the ombudsman examined the child's rights, the most common problems or factors that led to the need for a court to interfere with the family were: parents' alcohol disease - 52% , caring and educational negligence, including health and hygiene - 39%, violence, including physical, mental and sexual violence - 36%, difficult material situation of the family - 21% analyzed cases, lack of adequate parental supervision - 18% , parents' mental disorders - 12% and leaving the child under the care of another family member or hospital - 27%. It must be stressed that these problems and factors did not occur on their own. 88% There have been at least three of the problems examined jointly and 36% of the problems examined. The Ombudsman did not find that the main or exclusive circumstance resulting in the decision to place the child in foster care was the difficult material situation of the family. However, it is not possible to rule out cases of taking the child away from parents and putting in foster care because of family poverty. It is clear that, in situations where the child's life or health is at risk, placing in foster care is a means of safeguarding the child's well-being, but in the opinion of the Ombudsman, the financial situation of the family cannot be identified with a direct threat to the child's care and care situation. It should be pointed out with determination that parents' temporary parental failure due to financial failure and family poverty cannot result in a decision to take the child away from their parents. The prohibition to place in foster care only because of poverty is a guarantee that children will not be taken away from their parents because of the bad financial situation of the family, although I take this as a prevention. I also see this provision as an additional safeguard for the welfare of a child from poorer families, because it gives the State an obligation to help the family to remove this condition and improve its material status. ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-14-sejm-08-2.u220	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! This is the second time I've had the honour of presenting this project in the Parliament hall. It was presented for the first time in the previous term of the Sejm. On the other hand, based on the experience of the previous discussion, I think that first of all we need to explain a little bit closer what direct sales is, that it is not just a simple distribution channel, a way to sell goods and services. For many micro-entrepreneurs it is also a way to perform a professional job in life, to combine professional life with family life, a way of life, in short. Direct sales is a system of distribution of goods and services, which consists in direct contact between the trader and the consumer. Do not confuse this form of sales with telemarketing or telesales. This is not an unwanted telephone contact, that is sales, which consists in providing the consumer also with support for what product to choose, what product to best use, what are the properties of this product, advantages, and support for after-sales service. The relationship between the consumer and the trader who sells the product to him often occurs within the organized sales structures that have been operating in Poland for many years, and there is a need not only to disseminate the knowledge of consumer rights that consumers have in this regard, but also to disseminate knowledge about the sales systems themselves. Therefore, the intention of the creators of this project is not to establish the day of the seller, consultant or consumer, but the intention is to establish the Day of Direct Selling, direct sales which corresponds, if we look at the statutory regulations, the definition of sales outside the premises of the company. Off-premises sales are characterised by the fact that the transaction takes place on the street, at the consumer's home, it does not take place at the premises. What, on the other hand, is important? In direct sales, the main focus of the seller, who acts ethically, puts on maintaining relations with the consumer, building his image and on the most reliable and ethical relationship with the consumer, economic relationship of course. In direct sales there are also values that allow us to realise how important it may be for the Polish economy, for the Polish market. Courtesy of the Polish Direct Sales Association today I can say that in 2014, because for 2015 results are not available, the value of transactions made in direct sales in Poland is around 36 million transactions, and the cash value - 2886 million PLN. Of course, I'm talking about the total value of all these transactions. For at least a few years in Poland in direct sales systems, nearly 900 thousand have been involved in the distribution systems of goods and services. persons. These are both micro-entrepreneurs, so-called. self-employed people and consumers, and these people really benefit from these systems. They not only make it possible to acquire quality products and services, to obtain support for the use of these products, but also offer a chance of some flexibility in employment. This is extremely important for micro-entrepreneurs because, as part of self-employment, they can themselves decide how they want to present their offer to consumers when they want to do so, and this flexibility is very important for those who can in any way be more difficult to access the labour market. As far as statistics are concerned, for example, it can be pointed out that 87% Sellers in this system are women. In the case of the 50+ age group, close to 30% of those who are among Polish sellers belong to this age group. And one more important thing: more than 30% of sellers who start their business in this system are those who have not been professional before. All of this shows how important it is for the sales form and the branch of the economy or even the industry for the Polish economy, for the Polish labour market. This method of sale enables not only professional activation, not only access to quality products, but also professional self-realization for many people who have not found this possibility in traditional employment. It is therefore worth promoting this form of sales. On the other hand, it may seem at least interesting, why nearly 120 thousand. people supported this project - 120 thousand. I would like to thank the people I am grateful for submitting my signatures to. These people are not only consultants, they are not only consumers who buy in this distribution channel, but also those who understand the system and appreciate its advantages. In order to explain why the authors of this project want to give a statutory rank to an event which has been organized in Poland for several years with the support of the Polish Direct Sales Association, one has to say that knowledge about direct sales in Poland is not sufficient. We are talking here not only about the knowledge of consumer rights, which is sometimes harmed by unethical entrepreneurs, but non-ethical entrepreneurs operate in every sector of the economy, and it is not possible through the prism of such unethical practices to assess the whole industry, the industry as a whole, and the intention of the creators of this project is to bring about a situation in which this knowledge will have a solid basis for spreading it to society, a situation in which the group of people concerned will disseminate it, in order to enable consumers, entrepreneurs, market participants to understand what this form of sales is about better protecting and implementing their rights, because our assessment is not just about fixing the effects of non-ethical entrepreneurs who in some way harm the consumer, but also about how to educate the consumer. This will probably reduce the cost of support to such consumers, the cost of legal aid which must therefore be granted. The establishment of a Direct Selling Day would certainly contribute to the creation of an appropriate framework, to giving a certain rank to such an event. I stress once again: Direct Sales Day, not the seller, not the consultant. This initiative does not diminish the role of any other profession or any professional group that operates in Poland. This is an initiative that aims to promote certain values, ethical values in sales and which aims to ensure that there are appropriate conditions for this. If we would like to explain why on October 17th, we must refer to these events that I have already mentioned. On the other hand, focusing on what this law can actually give the industry itself and all Poles apart from the dissemination of knowledge, it can actually also refer to the values of professional activation that I mentioned, because it can not only attract people who are interested in starting their own business, but also as a unique initiative on a European and global scale can attract certain entrepreneurs to invest in Poland, to invest in this industry, which will create new jobs. And that is a question that also needs to be underlined. The law itself consists of only two articles. This is not a complicated act. What has been written in the bill and in its justification illustrates the sincere intentions of the creators of the project. It does not entail hidden risks, there are no complicated provisions on which interpretation doubts would arise, and it is certainly not subject to regulation of European Union law, nor will it have negative consequences for the state budget. For this reason, I ask the High House to support this project, to support this citizens' initiative, which, as I said, is more than 115 thousand. people, almost 120 thousand. the people supported it. In my opinion, such an initiative cannot be left indifferent. It is all the more worthy of support because it illustrates how many people are interested in this industry, its development and dissemination of knowledge in this area. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-14-sejm-08-2.u224	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Dear Representatives of the Legislative Initiative Committee of the draft law establishing on 17 October on the Day of Direct Sales! On behalf of the Civic Platform Club, I have the honour to present my position on the project submitted. Ladies and gentlemen! Direct Sales Day has been successfully organized regularly for 4 years, i.e. since 2012, by the Polish Direct Sales Association, which is also the main initiator of this citizens' initiative. These celebrations take the form of a thematic conference involving state institutions, implementing an information policy on state support for direct retail sales. The Office for Competition and Consumer Protection plays a particular role in the annual event, which must inform citizens of their rights and protect against the dangers of unfair commercial practices. The applicant justifies the introduction of a legislative initiative by the fact that the organisation of conferences and meetings of the Direct Sales Day is a response to the needs of sellers and companies active in the direct sales industry. So let's take a look at this industry. Direct sales, as defined, are to offer goods and services directly to consumers, on an individual basis, usually at a customer's home, workplace or other place, other than fixed retail outlets. I'm sure we've all come across this form of sales. Direct selling is a flexible way of earning. In Poland about 85% Sellers treat her as extracurricular activities. Almost 90% direct-selling workers are women who are more likely than men to agree to work with housekeeping and childcare. Women are undoubtedly the driving force of the entire industry, whose main asset is moving working time. In 2014, 910 thousand cooperated with direct sales companies. persons. And now in terms of products, among direct sales companies operating on the Polish market there are both small and large companies, with annual turnover measured in hundreds of millions of PLN. Some large companies are international corporations, e.g. Amway, Zepter, Oriflame, Herbalife, Mary Kay, Avon and many others. For many years, cosmetics have been leading in direct sales, although their sales are gradually decreasing from year to year. And so in 2014 it was 67% , on subsequent positions there were dietary supplements - 10% , household appliances - 8% , clothing and accessories - 6%, services - 5% and economic chemistry - 1%. After this brief look at the industry, it is time to look at the needs of customers using direct industry services. The establishment of a Direct Sales Day through the adoption of a law by the Polish Parliament obliges applicants to see this trade holiday in a broad perspective, where in particular the social educational aspect should be taken into account and awareness-raising of customers' citizens about both benefits and potential risks, such as unfair practices of manipulation and inducing the purchase of low quality products at a high price. We must remember that older people, unaware and misled by companies offering various products, are most often the prey of dishonest direct sellers. When we are concerned about the welfare and financial security of Polish citizens, the Day of Direct Selling could accept the name of the day of the informed customer of direct sellers. Then it could be considered that this day will contribute to an increase in knowledge about the mechanisms of direct sales in Poland. We cannot talk about the nationwide celebrations of Direct Sales Day without clear socio-information action, without real, reliable information to citizens about threats and legal tools protecting their domestic budgets from companies that often count on gullibility. But it's a $100,000 meeting. signatures under the citizens' bill always deserve praise for the established Legislative Initiative Committee. We appreciate it and congratulate you. It will also be an expression of the state's observation of the activity, which it carries out in Poland over 900 thousand. people, many of whom are in high risk groups in terms of opportunities in the labour market and who do not wait passively for help, only organise themselves, taking their own fate into their own hands. In this way, they not only actively shape their future, but also relieve public finances and are living proof that entrepreneurship does not have to be limited to groups with which it is stereotypically associated. ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-14-sejm-08-2.u244	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen of the Ministers! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Members of the applicants a draft law amending the Food Safety and Nutrition Act and certain other laws. I would like to remind you that this is not a new project, this project was already being examined at the meeting of the Sejm on 23 September 2015 in the previous term, it was addressed to the committee, unfortunately, due to the end of the term of office of the Sejm it was not finally adopted. I would like to make it clear that this project mainly concerns matters relating to direct sales. Let me remind you that, with regard to the current condition for direct sales, which we have also just mentioned in the previous point, we have a situation where products which have not been processed from the holding can be sold in certain quantities without tax, while products processed after the amendment of the Personal Income Tax Act can be sold at a certain value, i.e. up to around 600 thousand. PLN per year, 150 thousand. Euros, for the so-called a flat-rate tax, a 2% income tax on the usual declaration of this processing and this production, and in a book entry or any other register which the farmer, processor, seller will be able to keep and perform. [[Once a day.]] A year, of course. I would also like to say that, in addition to these two sales systems, there is still the possibility of marketing, but when registering economic activities, processed agricultural products from the farm within the framework of the so-called. marginal production, limited local production. Today, more than 2 000 are involved in this activity in Poland. farms, already known as enterprises, because they get this status after registration. In this draft law, ladies and gentlemen, coming up against these solutions, which we have just adopted in the Personal Income Tax Act adopted in the previous parliamentary term on 9 April 2015, we want to put security oversight issues in order, because also EU legislation, and national legislation, shows that when it comes to food marketing, food sales, consumer safety is paramount and that both national and EU legislation is very strict in this respect. Many times, not only in this House, but also in various discussions in the field, the question of why food products, often produced in a particular village, live tens or hundreds of kilometres, are processed, packaged and often later returned to this producer of raw materials, a farmer, a consumer, staying a long way for a long time. So here, when it comes to direct sales, when we are discussing the possibility of increasing profitability in agriculture, it is important that on this farm where the product is produced, it can be processed and sold, whether it is sold directly on the farm, whether it is sold on the nearest market, so that individual links can be eliminated from this chain of intermediaries and that on the one hand the consumer receives a product of known origin, fresh, desirable quality, and on the other hand, so that the producer can make part of the added value obtained by intermediaries or processors, enter into his farm, leave his family. Thus, often such a fundamental obstacle to the taking up of direct sales, direct sales activity was precisely the need to register this activity, to keep the whole system of financial records of such activities carried out within the framework of, for example, MOL, food safety and control regulations. To date, the issue of supervision of direct sales, the preparation of these products on the farm, i.e. their processing, is carried out by sanitary inspection, but the registration of the holding, registration of this production is carried out in the Veterinary Inspection. The point is not to cause this fear to farmers of a few institutions that control the farm. It is often said by farmers that, above all, we are afraid of the multitude of checks that are carried out on a farm that decides on processing and direct sales, we expect this to be sorted out. This draft law transfers the competence of health inspection to the Veterinary Inspection so that a farmer with that veterinary district inspector who knows his farm can undertake or carry out his work on an ongoing basis. This inspector, of course, not only controls, but also recommends, supervises this production, and what is important, also determines the conditions when there are any external epidemiological threats, also carries out this strict control and surveillance. Thus, there is no concern as regards consumers that consumers can obtain a product of untested quality and, on the other hand, this possibility of direct relations and contact of the veterinarian, Veterinary Inspection, producer and later consumer also has its value. I still want to highlight one important element. Well, a certain relationship was historically established between the consumer and the producer. The consumer who will benefit from production on the farm and direct sales can be directly checked under what conditions and how the preparation or production of the food product he uses is carried out. This is also a value that also raises the kind of production-production awareness of the manufacturer and supplier himself. Moreover, the market is growing rapidly, and special consumer cooperatives are already being established in cities, which expect on the other hand such a supply and a producer of traditional, simple food produced directly on the farm, with a known origin. I think that this solution, this change, these provisions are conducive to this type of approach to the food sector and the possibility of processing and selling on the farm. ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-14-sejm-08-2.u247	Thank you very much. I would ask Mr Leszek Ruszczyk to speak on behalf of the Civic Platform Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-14-sejm-08-2.u248	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform has the honour to present its position on the draft law amending the Food Safety and Nutrition Act and some other laws, print No 170. The proposed amendments aim to clarify the competence of the State Sanitary Inspection and Veterinary Inspection for the supervision of food-producing entities and retailing their products. Thanks to the changes introduced, the Veterinary Inspection becomes an official food control body competent to supervise food safety over farmers' retail and production establishments. Farmers can therefore produce and process products of animal origin in order to sell them to direct consumers, i.e. to the final consumer. Farmers can, by way of clarification in the Food Safety Supervision Act, produce locally, shorten the supply chain and intermediaries, and sell fresh food directly to the benefit of consumers. To date, these supervisory powers have been assigned to the State Sanitary Inspection in the field of retail trade. The veterinary inspection, having experience in the supervision of production holdings, the production of food of animal origin and its placing on the market, with the staff of official veterinarians, is as competent as possible to carry out such supervision. It is therefore reasonable for the authorities of that inspection to also supervise the production and processing of animal products by allowing them to be retailed. The aim of the proposed amendment is to establish precisely this supervisory competence for the Veterinary Inspections on the safety of food production and retailing and to enable farmers to directly trade their products in home establishments or markets. However, in view of the broadening of competences, we see the need to increase the expenditure on the Veterinary Inspection in order to secure the financing of possible new jobs. Seeing the appropriateness of introducing changes in food and nutrition safety oversight and facilitating farmers' control of the production and sale of fresh food sold to direct consumers, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club supports the project and is in favour of further proceding the above law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
