ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-14-sejm-08-2.u312	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Commission has examined a law amending the Police Act and several other laws, among others. Act on Internal Security Agency and Intelligence Agency, military services, Customs Service or Military Police. Our work was based on the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal, which ordered several changes in July 2014 at the request of the Ombudsman and the Prosecutor General. It was a bill... The issue that we have debated is quite important, because on the one hand we are concerned with the security of citizens and the need to ensure that Poles, citizens of the Republic of Poland live safe lives in times which, as we know, are not the safest at the moment, and on the other hand there is a second value, which is also very important and which we must remember, that is the freedom of citizens, the right to privacy. The Constitutional Tribunal considered that the existing law had been incompatible with several articles of the Constitution for many years and ordered a change. That date was set by the Court for 6 February 2016 by the Court of First Instance. At that time, the Sejm could or should have changed it. In the previous term, this law was not changed, a subcommittee was set up for this purpose, but it did not complete its work. There was a senate project that could be worked on, but the committee did not complete the work on the project, so we had to lean on it, having of course less time and bearing in mind that if we did not adopt these changes in accordance with the ruling of the tribunal by 6 February, then the police would be helpless when it comes to certain important aspects in certain important areas. Maybe in a few sentences, which concerned the issues under appeal to the tribunal or what the judgment of the tribunal was about. First, the Court found that the provisions concerning the application of operational control by the ABW in the case of offences which undermine the economic basis of the State were incompatible with the Constitution. The Court considered that such a definition was too broad and should be clarified. The Court found it incompatible with the Constitution that the rules on the possibility for the services to collect data not requiring the consent of the court did not provide for an independent review of the availability of the data. That's another thing. The next thing that is not in accordance with the Constitution are provisions concerning the collection by the services of data which do not require the consent of the court in so far as they do not provide for the destruction of data which is not relevant to the conduct of the proceedings. Another thing - the provisions on operational control regarding the guarantee of immediate commission and protocol destruction of materials containing information covered by the prohibition of evidence on which the court has not repealed professional secrecy or the repeal was inadmissible are incompatible with the Constitution. Those are the main points. The Commission has appointed a subcommittee from its membership. Yesterday was the work of the subcommittee, and today was the work of the committee. What are the effects? The results are that in the Police Act and in those laws I mentioned, we execute the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal. And so, first of all, the project introduces a follow-up control of the data collection process by the competent district court. This has not been the case so far, and this is what the tribunal has been commenting on, considering that this is not in accordance with the Constitution. Now this issue has been written down in this bill. Moreover, in the new law, this amendment states that the Head of Service is required to keep a record of the data collected and to provide the competent court with information on the information collected every 6 months. Another thing. The court shall carry out an inspection of the procedure for the collection of data by the services, may refer to the material of the proceedings. Next thing. The Minister of Justice shall report annually, on the basis of the data obtained from the courts, to the Sejm of the Republic of Poland on the data received by the data services. In the current law, it has not been clarified whether there are no regulations at all that would tell us what to do with telecommunication, postal and internet data that have been collected by services and which are not useful and have not been transferred to criminal proceedings. At the moment, in accordance with the judgment of the Constitutional Court, they must be destroyed. The Head of Service shall issue an order at the end of such proceedings, i.e. a description of the procedure which requires the protocolal, committee-based destruction of these data. We also introduce very important issues - the bill introduces a procedure for dealing with materials obtained during inspections, which may constitute evidence bans. This is primarily about the secret of confession and the defensive secret. Orders the heads of service immediately to destroy materials that may constitute an absolute ban on evidence. This was not the case in the existing law and the Constitutional Tribunal also ordered such provisions to be introduced. In the draft law adopted by the committee, there are also certain procedures for dealing with materials which may constitute a relative prohibition of evidence on which the court must give its consent in the proceedings. As I said earlier about these absolute bans of evidence which must be immediately destroyed, cannot be used at all, there is also a category of relative bans of evidence that can be used, but with the permission of the court. The Council of Ministers also presented its amendments to this project. Maybe I'll just mention the most important of these amendments. Here the government further clarified whether in its position the government clarified the provisions concerning the prohibition of evidence, which I had just mentioned, also narrowed down the grounds for obtaining data by the police, the Military Police, the Border Guard, the Customs Service, the records explaining the doubts that the collection of data by services which do not require the permission of the court cannot concern the control of the content of correspondence. There's been a big discussion here too, and there's been a lot of misrepresentations lately, and there's been some misconceptions. Telecommunications data that does not require court's consent, and the content of correspondence, whether online or postal or telecommunications, which was, is and will be able to be used... The services will have access to it, but only with the permission of the court. So please remember that these are two... that if the services want to get the content of someone's phone calls or text messages, the content of correspondence, whether it's mail or e-mail correspondence, they'll have to have the court's prior consent, not the subsequent one, but the preliminary consent of the court. And nothing changes here. Therefore, the fear that there will be some mass surveillance of Internet users is unfounded. As I said at the outset, this law is the execution of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal which imposed... In fact, that case should be dealt with by the previous Sejm, but he didn't make it. Surely the course of discussions on this law, and on the public, and on the committee, indicates that it is not an easy law, because, as I mentioned, we have issues of citizens' security, public security, and on the other we have issues of the right to privacy, the right to intimacy, human rights. And these two planes must meet. It's not like by increasing, I don't know, security, there won't be freedom. I agree here with what Minister Mariusz Kaminski said on the committee that in order to be a free man, one must also be a safe man. Examples of recent terrorist attacks in Paris show us that what if someone can go to a restaurant, go to the Champs Elysées, if they leave the house, is not sure if they will be safe there, whether there will be another assassination, because the services of his country, his country, do not have any instruments to ensure this safety. So, in my view, this law protects individuals' rights, citizens' rights, the right to privacy, and on the other hand it gives the services certain opportunities or certain competences primarily to - and this must also be made clear - the use of certain operational means not to all citizens, Mr X, Y, Z, but to those who committed the crime. So that this should also make it clear that it is not that this law is directed against all citizens, only these are certain mechanisms or powers that we give to the police or special services in their work to detect or prevent crimes. I want it to be clear. These are important things, because there was a message about Internet surveillance that suddenly the services would download some data that they hadn't done before. No, that's the fact that services have been allowed to download Internet data for many years, I'm just saying: Internet data, so as not to confuse it with the content of e-mails or other messages that we generate on the Internet. These records have existed for many years, and the service has been able to do so for the last eight years or even longer. On the other hand, the Constitutional Tribunal pointed out that there was no way to control whether the services needed these data, there was no control over what happens to them when they are useless. So we introduce in this law what the Constitutional Tribunal expected, first of all, if it turns out that the data collected by the services were unnecessary, they would be Commissionly destroyed, protocolally destroyed. Moreover, there is also judicial control over the data that the service was collecting. So that the court will have the right to see whether the services in question acted in accordance with the law. It's not like it's gonna disappear, because we're gonna make it, too, and there's laws that make the services register, create records of the data they've been downloading. So the court will then even be able to check how it was done, whether there were any violations of the law here. [[Mr President, are you an MEP or a committee rapporteur?]] Rapporteur. ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-14-sejm-08-2.u450	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In view of the ongoing discussion on changes in the boundaries of local self-government units and the establishment of new voivodships, acting on behalf of their own territorial structures of Law and Justice and on behalf of the inhabitants of the Lublin district, I would like to make a clear position on this matter. Lublin has been associated with Silesia since its uprising in the 13th century. On Lublin soil there were such singers of Silesia as Józef Lompa, Julius Ligon, Jan Nicodemus Jaron or Konstanty Damrot. It was here after World War I that the Silesian insurgents were active. The Śląsk Song and Dance Team, which is an ambassador of Silesian tradition and culture, is located in the district of Lublin Koscin. The identification of inhabitants of Lublin and Silesia is expressed in local names, guar, traditions and customs. Therefore, the membership of our county to the Silesian Voivodeship is a fundamental issue. This is a confirmation of his identity and 800-year history related to Upper Silesia. In 1976, as a result of the administrative reform carried out by the communist authorities, the district of Lublin was incorporated into the newly established Częstochowa voivodeship. This decision met with widespread disapproval, which was clearly expressed by the referendum held by the NSZZ.Solidarity in 1981, in which it was more than 90% voters voted in favour of returning to the Katowice voivodship. Only in 1998 did Lublin land return to the administrative borders of Upper Silesia. 17 years after those events plans for the return of the Częstochowa voivodship to the administrative map of the country appear. We do not deny such aspirations to the inhabitants of Częstochowa and culturally and historically connected with the spiritual capital of Poland, but we stand in the position that any administrative changes that might be made must take into account the will of the interested parties. No binding decisions can take place without the consent of the residents expressed through a wide public consultation, the opinions of local authorities, or the consent expressed through a referendum. Once again, I would like to stress that we are in favour of the further belonging of the Lublin district to the structures of the Śląskie voivodship. Thank you.

The case concerns the need to announce an offer contest and contract the benefits in the field of magnetic resonance imaging. The hospital has made great efforts to obtain funds for the purchase of an MRI, bearing in mind the well-being of the inhabitants of the city of Jaworzna and providing access to such research in the area of the commune and neighbouring towns. The Silesian Regional Branch of the NFZ has also repeatedly informed about these intentions and concrete actions. The assurance of funding of the research was submitted in this respect during the ceremony of opening the resonance studio of the then director of the Silesian institution. Unfortunately, after the MRI was launched - despite repeated attempts to communicate with the authorities of the Silesian Voivodeship Branch of the NFZ in writing and personally by the hospital directorate - no action has been taken by the fund to contract the above mentioned. benefits. Therefore, the inhabitants of Jaworzna are forced to use outpatient research in other voivodship centers, thus exposed to difficulties in the form of long commuting and long waiting time for such important research in order to establish a correct diagnosis and further treatment. This state of affairs is also so incomprehensible that recently the Silesian Regional Branch of the NFZ announces competitions dedicated to specific types of benefits or even specific benefits. The tender procedure for the purchase of medical equipment for SP ZOZ Multi-specialist Hospital in Jaworzno in order to improve the quality of highly specialised medicine - magnetic resonance was carried out within the framework of the RPO project of the Silesian Voivodeship for the years 2007 and 2013, priority IX: Health and recreation, announced and completed in 2011. The amount that was allocated to finance this task is almost PLN 5.5 million. It was financed in a proportion of 85% up to 15% Of which 15% were funds transferred from the grant of the town of Jaworzna. The hospital completed the reconstruction of the MRI building with its delivery on 31 December 2011. Unfortunately, since then, the NFZ has not announced a contest procedure for the MRI test, despite the fact that before the procedure for the purchase of the MRI, the hospital received an assurance from the NFZ that this procedure will be announced. High Izbo! In my opinion, it is unacceptable that residents of a 100-thousand city in which a public hospital, properly equipped, must travel to other cities of the voivodship to carry out such research. In this regard, I also referred in previous years to the NFZ as deputy president of the city and chairman of the social council of this hospital. I believe that the obvious pathology associated with the conduct of the Silesian Provincial Division of the NFZ in this area should be eliminated as soon as possible. I hope that the services referred to above will finally be contracted in the near future. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-14-sejm-08-2.u454	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Today's statement concerns a worrying change of connections from 1 February 2016 to 28 March from Katowice Airport in Pyrzowice to Warsaw-Okęcie Airport. This line is supported by Polish Airlines LOT and I am full of concerns that the scheduled connections will remain permanent for this time. Moreover, after the social and business talks, we came to the conclusion that it was pointless to cancel morning flights at hours. 9.05 from Katowice in months: February and March. The arguments in favour of leaving the morning flights at hours. 8.50 p.m. or 8.00 p.m. 9.05, depending on how the supporter of this line will want to plan, are the following. First of all, commercial arguments. For business, administration or politicians alike, leave at hours. 9 guarantees the possibility of dealing with all matters in the capital on one day and ensures the return to home on the same day. Commercial arguments are also related to the use of the current high attendance on this flight and the possibility of arriving at the airport from home at hours. 8.15, not hours. 5. Another commercial argument is also - with the departure option at hours. 5.45 - pushing and worsening the comfort of connections with the capital of existing passengers from hours. 9.05. The operational argument in turn is the much greater predictability of planned departures from Katowice in the morning with deep weather disturbances in the mornings at the airport in Krakow, especially during the autumn-winter period, as we observe on an ongoing basis. Departures, on the other hand, at hours. 5.45 from Katowice to Warsaw require that people arriving in Warsaw expect a minimum of 2, 3 hours. to open state institutions or business. According to the users of these connections, these are key arguments for leaving departures from Katowice to Warsaw around hours. 9, because it is the most favourable, and it is one of the largest regions in Poland. The connection to the capital is very desirable and checked so far. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-14-sejm-08-2.u456	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I wanted my statement today... I would like to present a situation that in many people arouses a lot of emotions, controversy, about the equivalent of money or the equivalent, of coal, which concerns miners. Here, in this Chamber, many times on this subject. Yeah, we did. Poland looking at Silesia and today, not even Silesia, but also other regions in the country where coal is extracted, has many reservations and comments about this. Let's take a look at the situation today. Well, we have, like, three groups of retirees and miners who, to varying degrees and under different conditions, benefit from the equivalent. First of all, we have the first group whose equivalent was secured by two laws. There is the Mining Act of 7 September 2007, which has been amended several times, which is actually applicable to liquidated mines. Both those miners and those who benefit from the Law on Money Equivalence for the right to free coal for persons entitled from mining companies - this is the Act of 6 July 2007 - benefit from the State budget allocated to the Social Security Office. More than PLN 180 million were saved for this purpose in the financial year 2016. We have a second group of mining pensioners who have so far benefited from the payment of this equivalent by coal companies. How's it looking today? Since 1 January 2015, these pensioners have been deprived of this equivalent by the Coal Company, by Jastrzębska Spółka Węglowa and... and not here. Katowice Holding Węglowy suspended from 1 January 2015 for 3 years payment of this equivalent. In turn we have three mining entities, which continue to pay this equivalent - Tauron Extraction, which pays for 3 tons of coal per year, Bogdanka mine and the Company for Restructuring of Mines. These are entities which in some way or directly 100% belong to the State Treasury or participate in capital also in the structures of the State. As of 1 January 2015, the group of 200, more than 220 thousand. persons, i.e. pensioners and miners, have been effectively deprived of the right to equivalent by the boards of coal companies and trade union boards. Of course, this was done for reasons related to the economic existence of these entities. But going back to the first point - some of these people benefit from a grant, from funding for this equivalent from the state budget. There is a third group of people who have not yet been secured have been deprived of this equivalent. These were workers of mining companies, who, also for economic reasons of their economic operators, gave up effectively, on the basis of decisions of management and trade unions, on this equivalent. All three groups, people worked hard underground, risking their lives, health and life at the same time under conditions of risk of methane explosion, carbon dust, dripping, mountaineer movement. All three groups worked in state-owned enterprises. Is it fair and fair to these people that some today enjoy this right and do not exercise it? Is this the fault of those who were deprived of it? I don't think so. Retirists and miners from Silesia and not only do they expect anything special, something extraordinary. They expect power not to divide them. They expect mining equivalent issues to be sorted out. Today, in thousands of pieces, they sue the regional courts, appeal to the boards of coal companies. [[♪ The bell ♪]] From this point on, I would like to appeal to the government, Prime Minister Szydło, for the government to take care of this matter as soon as possible and put it in order. It can't go on like this. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-14-sejm-08-2.u462	Statement on the 111th anniversary of Czesław Szczubałek's birth. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! January 7th this year We celebrated the 111th anniversary of Czesław Szczubałek's birth. I would therefore like to approximate the figure of the deceased during the uprising of the Warsaw lieutenant of the Polish Army, an officer of the Home Army, a doctor of medicine. Czesław Szczubałek was born on 7 January 1905 as the son of Stanisław and Agnieszka from the house of Rocławska. He attended high school in Lipna. During the Polish-Bolshevik War he volunteered to join the Polish Army, but because of his young age he was admitted only for a few weeks to serve in the field hospital. He was a member of the Polish Scouts Association. After completing the appropriate courses he conducted a sanitary course for scouts in Lipno. The high school prom exam passed. Stanisław Wyspiański in Mława. He was in charge of the platoon at the Military Adoption School Hufcu, studying there. After his graduation he started medical studies at the Faculty of Medicine at the University of Warsaw. He graduated from a medical doctor at the age of 28. In the meantime, he has received compulsory military training, receiving the rank of lieutenant of the reserve. Even before the outbreak of the war, he obtained a PhD in medical science. In August 1939, Czesław Szczubałek volunteered for military service. He was assigned to the bathroom in Powązki. After the war, this battalion was destroyed by the Germans. The puppet and some of the soldiers broke out of the lap, but soon he was captured by the Soviets. During his export to the USSR, he managed to escape from transport. Then he returned to the capital. Then he began working in a conspiracy. Czesław Szczubałek was part of the group of Warsaw women. In Sadyba he co-organized the group of the Polish Union troops. In 1942, this formation was merged with the National Army, acting from now on as 5. Division V of the Mokotów District of the Warsaw District of the National Army - battalion of the Oaza. The commandant of District 5 was Czesław Szczubałek ps. A lizard. After the outbreak of the Warsaw Uprising, he commanded the defense of Fort Czerniaków im. Jan Henryk Dąbrowski. On September 1, 1944, a bomb struck the corner chamber of the fort. Sadyba's insurgent command was killed then. Among the 24 officers and soldiers there were also Czesław Szubelek. Czesław Szczubelek was buried in Czerniakowski Cemetery, and then his remains were transferred to the Wilanów Cemetery, to the Oaza group headquarters. His name was the park around the street. Morszyńska and the moat of Fort Czerniakowski. Among the awards given to him are the Order of Virtuti Militari, the Cross of Valiant and the Golden Cross of Merit. Thank you.
